# Proposed OEIS comments: A302080 to A304855

One section per OEIS entry: the date the result was obtained, the paper that proves it, what the entry still records as unsettled, and the text proposed for posting on that entry.

**Nothing here has been posted.** See [../SUBMITTING.md](../SUBMITTING.md).

---

## A302081

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A302081>  
Result obtained: **1 September 2026**  
Paper: [3180](../papers/03001-03500/03180-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical for column k:
> Empirical for row n:

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical column recurrences are true. Each column counts arrays of a fixed width, and counting those a row at a time gives a transfer matrix, so every column satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most the number of states; checking the ones above is then a finite exact computation, and they hold. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

---

Result obtained: **31 August 2026**  
Paper: [7697](../papers/07501-08000/07697-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 3*a(n-1) -3*a(n-2) +6*a(n-3) -6*a(n-4) +3*a(n-5) -6*a(n-6) +a(n-7) -14*a(n-8) +9*a(n-9) +4*a(n-10) +2*a(n-11) -4*a(n-12) +2*a(n-13) +8*a(n-15) -4*a(n-16) for n>21

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 256 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 21. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A302524

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A302524>  
Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [11357](../papers/11001-11500/11357-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical recurrence of order 72 (see link above)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 72 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. Its order is 72, the order stated here, and so is the order of every tail. Any recurrence of that order the sequence satisfies therefore has the same characteristic polynomial, so it is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

Result obtained: **31 August 2026**  
Paper: [7447](../papers/07001-07500/07447-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 4*a(n-1) -8*a(n-3) -13*a(n-4) +20*a(n-5) +21*a(n-6) +4*a(n-7) -30*a(n-8) -44*a(n-9) -17*a(n-10) +72*a(n-11) +116*a(n-12) -20*a(n-13) -72*a(n-14) -52*a(n-15) -12*a(n-16) +24*a(n-17) +8*a(n-18) for n>19

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 64 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 19. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```
